\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:12}}{=}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}}.\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n,0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}\right)(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\left(\int_{[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}}\chi^{n}\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi^{n})\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}}.\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n,0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{0}}\right)(\mathbf{X}_{0})\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\left(\int_{[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}}\chi\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi)\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n}-\chi)\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\varrho(\chi^{n})-\varrho(\chi))\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right)\;,\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(81)

The last part can be rewritten as follows using equation (
73
),

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\exp\left(\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n}-\chi)\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}_{\mathbf{X}}^{U\overrightarrow{q}}+\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n}-\chi)(\mathrm{e}^{\chi}-1)\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\mathrm{e}^{\chi^{n}}-\chi^{n}-\mathrm{e}^{\chi}+\chi)\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n}-\chi)\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}_{\mathbf{X}}^{U\overrightarrow{q}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi-\chi^{n})\mathrm{d}(U\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}})\right)

We first handle the second integral above using (
79
) and the fact that
\varrho
is a non-negative function,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left|\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi-\chi^{n})\mathrm{d}(U\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}})\right|\right]\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0\;.

This together with Markov’s inequality lead to

\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi-\chi^{n})\mathrm{d}(U\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0\;,

(82)

and therefore, from
Méliot (2025, Proposition 1.4)
, there is a subsequence
\chi^{n_{k}}
such that

\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi-\chi^{n_{k}})\mathrm{d}(U\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}})\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0\;,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(83)

Furthermore, recall that
\varrho(a)=\mathrm{e}^{a}-a-1\geqslant a^{2}/2
, hence (
79
) can be used to control the stochastic integral w.r.t. the
\mathbb{P}
-martingale
\tilde{N}_{\mathbf{X}}^{U\overrightarrow{q}}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left(\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n}-\chi)\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}_{\mathbf{X}}^{U\overrightarrow{q}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{cor:ito_isometry}}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n}-\chi)^{2}\mathrm{d}(U\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt\leqslant 2\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi-\chi^{n})\mathrm{d}(U\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}})\right]\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0\;.

This, along with Markov’s inequality, results in

\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n}-\chi)\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}_{\mathbf{X}}^{U\overrightarrow{q}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0\;.

Combining this with (
82
) implies that

\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n}-\chi)\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}_{\mathbf{X}}^{U\overrightarrow{q}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi-\chi^{n})\mathrm{d}(U\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}})\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0\;.

As a consequence,
Méliot (2025, Proposition 1.4)
asserts that there is a subsequence
(\chi^{n_{k}})
such that

\left[\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n_{k}}-\chi)\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}_{\mathbf{X}}^{U\overrightarrow{q}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi-\chi^{n_{k}})\mathrm{d}(U\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}})\right]\xrightarrow{k\to\infty}0\;,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

It helps interpreting (
81
) as

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}}((\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n_{k}}}.\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n_{k},0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{0}}\right)(\mathbf{X}_{0})\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\left(\int_{[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}}\chi\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi)\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exp\left(\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\chi^{n_{k}}-\chi)\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(\varrho(\chi^{n_{k}})-\varrho(\chi))\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\eqref{eq:14}]{k\to\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\left(\int_{[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}}\chi\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\chi)\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right)\;,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

Replacing
\chi=\log U
, we arrive at our desired claim

\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}}((\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\left(\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\log U\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\log U)\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right)\;,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

Consequently, the
\mathrm{KL}
divergence reads as

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})=\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{0}|\overrightarrow{R}_{0})+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\log U\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\log U)\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right]\;.

Applying (
74
) to the function
\phi=\log U
, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{0}|\overrightarrow{R}_{0})+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}(U-1)\log U\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\log U)d\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{0}|\overrightarrow{R}_{0})+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}[(U-1)\log U-U+\log U+1]\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{0}|\overrightarrow{R}_{0})+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}h(U(t,x))\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)\right]\;,

with
h(a):=\varrho^{*}(a-1)=a\log a-a+1
for
a>0
. The proof of
Theorem

F.12
is then finished.
∎

In fact, we can simplify the
\mathrm{KL}
expression above by replacing
\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)=\sum_{y\in\mathsf{X}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbf{X}_{t-}\neq y}\overrightarrow{q}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},y)\delta_{y}(\mathrm{d}x)\mathrm{d}t

and
\overrightarrow{q}
by the formula given in (
59
) to arrive at

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{0}|\overrightarrow{R}_{0})+\mathbb{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{P}}\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}h(U(t,x))\sum_{y\in\mathsf{X}}\mathds{1}_{\mathbf{X}_{t-}\neq y}\overrightarrow{q}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},y)\delta_{y}(\mathrm{d}x)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{0}|\overrightarrow{R}_{0})+\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t-})))\mathrm{d}t\;.

F.2.2
Interpreting the time-reversed dynamic as a control-driven process

Let
\overrightarrow{R}\in\mathrm{MP}(\overrightarrow{q})
be the stationary measure on the path space
\mathbb{D}_{T_{f}}
introduced in the previous section. Then, the process
\overrightarrow{R}
is showed to be reversible, meaning
\overleftarrow{R}=\overrightarrow{R}
, and corresponds to the invariant measure
\gamma^{d}=\mathrm{Uniform}(\mathsf{X})
. Let
\overrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}=\mathrm{Law}((\overrightarrow{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})
represent the forward probability measure on the interval
\left[0,T_{f}\right]
starting from
{\mu^{\star}}
and governed by the forward generator
\overrightarrow{q}
given in (
59
). We denote the corresponding time-reversed probability measure ending at
\mu^{\star}
by
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}=\mathrm{Law}((\overleftarrow{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})
. Let
\mu_{t}
be the marginal density of the forward dynamic at time
t\in[0,T_{f}]
and denote by
\tilde{\mu}_{t}:=\mu_{t}/{\gamma^{d}}
the corresponding relative density.

Proposition F.15
.

The time reversal process
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
solves the Martingale Problem
\mathrm{MP}(u\overrightarrow{q})
with the control
u
given by: for
(t,x)\in[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}
,

u_{t}(x,y)=\begin{cases}{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}/{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(x)}\;,\quad&\text{if }y=\varphi^{(\ell)}(x)\text{ for some }\ell=1,\ldots,d\;,\\
{\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}u_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}/d\;,\quad&\text{if }y=x\;,\\
\hskip 56.9055pt1\;,\quad&\text{otherwise}\;.\end{cases}

(84)

Proof of
Proposition

F.15
.

Recall that the generator of the backward process
\overleftarrow{q}
satisfies the following equation for
t\in[0,T_{f}]
and
x\neq y
,

\overleftarrow{q}_{t}(x,y)=\frac{\mu_{T_{f}-t}(y)}{\mu_{T_{f}-t}(x)}\overrightarrow{q}(y,x)\;.

Using the fact that the stationary distribution
\gamma^{d}
satisfies the following balance equation for
x,y\in\mathsf{X}
,
x\neq y
,

\gamma^{d}(x)\overrightarrow{q}(x,y)=\gamma^{d}(y)\overrightarrow{q}(y,x)\;,

we can express the backward generator as the perturbation of the forward one as follows

\overleftarrow{q}_{t}(x,y)=\frac{\mu_{T_{f}-t}(y)}{\mu_{T_{f}-t}(x)}\overrightarrow{q}(y,x)=\frac{\mu_{T_{f}-t}(y)\gamma^{d}(x)}{\mu_{T_{f}-t}(x)\gamma^{d}(y)}\overrightarrow{q}(x,y)=\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(y)}{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(x)}\overrightarrow{q}(x,y)\;.

Note that
\overrightarrow{q}(x,y)=0
for
y\notin\{x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x);\;\ell=1,\ldots,d\}
, thus we can define the control
u
as: for
(t,x)\in[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}
,

u_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))=\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(x)}\quad\text{ for }\ell=1,\ldots,d\;,\quad\text{and}\quad u_{t}(x,y)=1\quad\text{for }y\notin\{x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x);\;\ell=1,\ldots,d\}\;,

to obtain the relation

\overleftarrow{q}_{t}(x,y)=u_{t}(x,y)\overrightarrow{q}(x,y)\;,\quad\text{for }(t,x,y)\in[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}^{2}\text{ and }y\neq x\;.

Furthermore, under the convention

u_{t}(x,x)=\frac{-\sum_{y\neq x}u_{t}(x,y)\overrightarrow{q}(x,y)}{\overrightarrow{q}(x,x)}=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}u_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\;,\quad\text{for }(t,x)\in[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}\;,

u\overrightarrow{q}
in fact forms a generator and satisfies
\overleftarrow{q}=u\overrightarrow{q}
, which implies that
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}\in\mathrm{MP}(u\overrightarrow{q})
and we conclude the proof.
∎

Proposition

F.15
shows that the unique control
u
associated with the backward dynamic
(\overleftarrow{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]}
in Girsanov’s thereom
F.12
is in fact Markovian. This enables expressing the Radon-Nikodym density
\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}/\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}
as

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}}((\overleftarrow{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\overleftarrow{X}_{0})\exp\left(\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\log u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t-},x)\mathrm{d}\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\log u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t-},x))\mathrm{d}\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}\right)\;,

where
u
is explicitly given in (
84
).

F.2.3
Evolution of the control in the reversed-time system

This section aims to characterize the control corresponding to the backward dynamics through the Hamilton–Jacobi–Bellman (HJB) equation. This characterization serves as the key ingredient for applying Itô’s formula to analyze the evolution of the time-reversed process.

Proposition F.16

(Hamilton–Jacobi–Bellman equation)
.

The control
u
given in (
84
) admits the following formula

\displaystyle u_{t}(x,y)=\mathrm{e}^{V(t,x)-V(t,y)}\;,\quad\text{for }(t,x,y)\in[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}^{2}\;,\quad x\neq y\;,

where
V(t,x)=-\log\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(x)
, which satisfies the following HJB equation

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}V(t,x)=\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}[\mathrm{e}^{V(t,x)-V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}-1]\;,\\
V(T_{f},x)=g(x)=-\log\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}}{\mathrm{d}\gamma^{d}}(x)\;,\end{cases}\quad\text{for }(t,x)\in[0,T_{f})\times\mathsf{X}\;.

(85)

Proof of
Proposition

F.16
.

Denote
V(t,x):=-\log\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(x)
, then the optimal control can be interpreted as

u_{t}(x,y)=\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(y)}{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(x)}=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-V(t,y)}}{\mathrm{e}^{-V(t,x)}}=\mathrm{e}^{V(t,x)-V(t,y)}\;,\quad\text{for }(t,x,y)\in[0,T_{f}]\times\mathsf{X}^{2}\text{ and }x\neq y\;.

In addition, the function
V
fulfills the following equation: for
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathsf{X}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}V(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial_{t}\mu_{T_{f}-t}(x)}{\mu_{T_{f}-t}(x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathsf{X}}\mu_{T_{f}-t}(y)\overrightarrow{q}(y,x)}{\mu_{T_{f}-t}(x)}\quad\text{(by forward Kolmogorov equation)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathsf{X}}\mu_{T_{f}-t}(y)\overrightarrow{q}(x,y)\gamma^{d}(x)/\gamma^{d}(y)}{\mu_{T_{f}-t}(x)}\quad\text{(by balance equation)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in\mathsf{X}}\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(y)}{\tilde{\mu}_{T_{f}-t}(x)}\overrightarrow{q}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}[\mathrm{e}^{V(t,x)-V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}-1]\;.

Moreover,
V
also satisfies the final condition

\displaystyle V_{T_{f}}(x)=-\log\tilde{\mu}_{0}(x)=-\log\tilde{\mu}^{\star}(x)=g(x)\;,\quad\text{for }x\in\mathsf{X}\;,

therefore
V
solves the HJB equation (
85
) and thus concludes the proof of
Proposition

F.16
.
∎

To derive the convergence bound, it is essential to interpret the evolution of the control using the HJB equation first. In fact, the control above satisfies the following martingale and monotone property due to its characterization given in
Proposition

F.16
.

Proposition F.17
.

With all the notations above,
u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))
is a
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
-martingale for fixed
\ell=1,\ldots,d
. Consequently,
h(u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t})))
is a
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
-submartingale and the monotonicity follows:

\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}}[h(u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))]\leqslant\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}}[h(u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t})))]\;,\quad\text{ for }\quad 0\leqslant s\leqslant t\leqslant T_{f}\;.

Proof of
Proposition

F.17
.

Fix
t\in[0,T_{f}]
and
\ell=1,\ldots,d
,
applying Itô’s formula on

f^{\ell}(t,\overleftarrow{X}_{t}):=u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))=\mathrm{e}^{V(t,\overleftarrow{X}_{t})-V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))}\;,

and note that
\text{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}_{.})=\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
solves
\mathrm{MP}(u\overrightarrow{q})
as well as
\overleftarrow{X}_{t}=\overleftarrow{X}_{t-}
for Lebesgue almost all
t\in(0,T_{f}]

(see
Mozumder, 2009
, Proposition 2.1)
, we obtain that the process

\displaystyle f^{\ell}(t,\overleftarrow{X}_{t})-f^{\ell}(0,\overleftarrow{X}_{0})-\int_{0}^{t}\left[\partial_{s}f^{\ell}(s,\overleftarrow{X}_{s})+(u\overrightarrow{q})f^{\ell}_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})\right]\mathrm{d}s

is a
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
-martingale.
Denote

\displaystyle b_{s}^{\ell}:=\partial_{s}f^{\ell}(s,\overleftarrow{X}_{s})+(u\overrightarrow{q})f^{\ell}_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}),\quad\text{for }s\in\left[0,t\right]\;.

We aim to prove that
b^{\ell}_{s}=0
. Indeed, by the definition of
f^{\ell},\overrightarrow{q}
and the HJB equation (
85
), we get that

\displaystyle b_{s}^{\ell}

\displaystyle=u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))\left[\partial_{s}V(s,\overleftarrow{X}_{s})-\partial_{s}V(s,\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left[u_{s}(\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}),\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))-u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))\right]\lambda u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))

\displaystyle=\lambda u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))-\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{s}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}),\varphi^{(i)}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left[u_{s}(\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}),\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))-u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))\right]\lambda u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Big[u_{s}(\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}),\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-u_{s}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}),\varphi^{(i)}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))u_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))\Big]\;.

Using the identity
u_{s}(x,\varphi^{(i)}(x))=\mathrm{e}^{V(s,x)-V(s,\varphi^{(i)}(x))}
for
i=1,2,\ldots,d
in
Proposition

F.22
yields

\displaystyle b^{\ell}_{s}

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Bigg[\mathrm{e}^{V(s,\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))-V(s,\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))+V(s,\overleftarrow{X}_{s})-V(s,\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\mathrm{e}^{V(s,\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))-V(s,\varphi^{(i)}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))+V(s,\overleftarrow{X}_{s})-V(s,\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left[\mathrm{e}^{-V(s,\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))+V(s,\overleftarrow{X}_{s})}-\mathrm{e}^{-V(s,\varphi^{(i)}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s})))+V(s,\overleftarrow{X}_{s})}\right]=0\;,

as
\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))=\varphi^{(i)}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s}))
for any
\ell,i=1,\ldots,d
. We thus conclude that
u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))
is a
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
-martingale. Furthermore, since
h
is convex, it follows that
h(u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t})))
is a
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
-submartingale, which implies the desired monotonicity for
\ell=1,2,\ldots,d
and concludes the proof.

∎

F.3
Connection between the transition matrix and canonical process point of view

As we see in previous sections, the time reversal process can be understood not only via the backward transition matrix but also via the process driven by the control. The transition matrix point of view provides an approximation of the score to simulate the backward process, which is very useful in practice. In parallel, the canonical process point of view gives us a better understanding of the evolution of the time reversal process, which allows us to show a theoretical guarantee on our algorithm. These two points of view in fact have a strong relation, which will be specified in the following Proposition.

Proposition F.18
.

The control
u
driving the backward process satisfies the following relation w.r.t. the score function defined in (
17
) as

\displaystyle u_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))

\displaystyle=1-s^{\ell}_{t}(x)\;,\quad\text{with }\ell=1,\ldots,d\text{ and }(t,x)\in[0,T_{f})\times\mathsf{X}\;.

(86)

Proof of
Proposition

F.18
.

The result follows directly from the definition of the score function
s
in (
17
) and the form of the control
u
in (
84
). This identity confirms the equivalence between the transition matrix viewpoint and the canonical process formulation.
∎

F.4
Optimal control perspective on the time-reversed process

This section presents an alternative perspective on deriving the HJB equation, viewing it through the lens of optimal control and leveraging the Dynamic Programming Principle. In particular, the predictable process

u
defined in
Theorem

F.12
can be characterized not only via the backward generator but also as the solution to an optimal control problem involving the expression of relative entropy.

In the continuous case,
Conforti et al. (2025)
demonstrated that the time reversal process can be formulated as a solution to an optimal control problem. This characterization not only describes the dynamics of the process but also serves as a powerful framework for deriving the HJB equation, which can be obtained by leveraging Girsanov’s theorem (
Theorem

F.12
)

Let
\overrightarrow{R}
be the stationary measure on the path space
\mathbb{D}_{T_{f}}
introduced in the previous section. Then, the process
\overrightarrow{R}
is reversible, meaning
\overleftarrow{R}=\overrightarrow{R}
, and corresponds to the invariant measure
\gamma^{d}=\mathrm{Uniform}(\mathsf{X})
. Let
\overrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
represent the forward probability measure on the interval
\left[0,T_{f}\right]
starting from
{\mu^{\star}}
and governed by the forward generator
\overrightarrow{q}
given in (
59
). We denote the corresponding backward probability measure ending at
\mu^{\star}
by
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
.

Proposition F.19
.

The time reversal process
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
satisfies the following optimization problem

\displaystyle\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}=\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{D}_{T_{f}}):~\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})<\infty}\left(\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})+\int g\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{T_{f}}\right),\quad\text{with }g=-\log\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}}{\mathrm{d}\gamma^{d}}\;.

Proof of
Proposition

F.19
.

For
\mathbb{P}=\mathrm{Law}((\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}])}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{D}_{T_{f}})
such that
\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})<\infty
, the Donsker-Varadhan variational formulation of the KL implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})=\sup_{f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{P})\text{ s.t. }\int\mathrm{e}^{f}\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}<\infty}\left(\int f\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}-\log\int\mathrm{e}^{f}\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}\right)\;.

Taking
f((\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})=-g({\mathbf{X}}_{T_{f}})
yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})

\displaystyle\geqslant\int-g\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{T_{f}}-\log\int\mathrm{e}^{-g}\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{T_{f}}=-\int g\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{T_{f}}-\log\int\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}=-\int g\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{T_{f}}\;,

since
\int\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}=1
. On the other hand, since
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
is the backward process ended at
{\mu^{\star}}
and
\overrightarrow{R}
is a reversible path probability measure on
\left[0,T_{f}\right]
,
i.e.
,
\overrightarrow{R}=\overleftarrow{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}}((\overleftarrow{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}}{\mathrm{d}\gamma^{d}}(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}})=\mathrm{e}^{-g(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}})}\;.

This implies

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}|\overrightarrow{R})=\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\gamma^{d})=\int\log\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}}{\mathrm{d}\gamma^{d}}\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}=-\int g\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}=-\int g\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}_{T_{f}}\;.

Combining the previous results, we obtain that the time reversal
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
is the optimal solution to the following problem

\displaystyle\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}=\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{\mathbb{P}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{D}_{T_{f}}):~\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})<\infty}\left(\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})+\int g\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{T_{f}}\right)\;,

which is the desired conclusion.
∎

Utilizing the expression for
\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}|\overrightarrow{R})
given by Girsanov’s Theorem
F.12
, we can now frame the corresponding Optimal Control problem.

Theorem F.20
.

Denote by
\mathcal{D}
the set of all
U:\mathbb{D}_{T_{f}}\times\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}\to[0,\infty)
satisfying the integrability condition

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\varrho^{*}(|U_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}^{U}_{[0,t)},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t-}^{U}))-1|)\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty\;,

which is indeed equivalent to condition (
61
). Then
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{\star}}
is the law of
\overleftarrow{X}^{u^{*}}
with
u^{*}
is the optimal solution to

\displaystyle\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{[0,t)}^{U},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t-}^{U})))\mathrm{d}t+g(\overleftarrow{X}^{U}_{T_{f}})\right]\;,

(87)

\displaystyle\text{s.t. }\mathrm{Law}((\overleftarrow{X}_{t}^{U})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})\in\mathrm{MP}(U\overrightarrow{q})\;,\text{with }(U\overrightarrow{q})(\omega,t,x,y):=U_{t}(\omega_{[0,t)},y)\overrightarrow{q}(x,y)\text{ for }x\neq y\;.

Proof of
Theorem

F.20
.

Theorem
F.20
is a consequence of Theorem
F.12
and Proposition
F.19
.
∎

F.4.1
Hamilton–Jacobi–Bellman equation

The goal of this section is to derive the Hamilton–Jacobi–Bellman (HJB) equation as in
Proposition

F.16
using the optimal control viewpoint. To this purpose, we first consider the generalization of the previous control problem. Let
J
be the following cost

\displaystyle J(t,x,U)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda\int_{[t,T_{f}]}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{[t,s)}^{t,x,U},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s-}^{t,x,U})))\mathrm{d}s+g(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}}^{t,x,U})\right]\;,

\displaystyle\text{s.t. }\begin{cases}\text{Law}((\overleftarrow{X}_{t}^{t,x,U})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})\in\mathrm{MP}(U\overrightarrow{q})\;,\\
\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,U}_{t-}=x\;,\end{cases}\quad\text{for }(x,t,U)\in\mathsf{X}\times\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathcal{D}\;.

Consider
V(t,x)
to be the value function of the previous cost function,
i.e.
,

\displaystyle V(t,x):=\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}J(t,x,U)\;.

The following Dynamic Programming Principle is the main tool to derive the HJB equation.

Lemma F.21
.

(
Touzi, 2012
, Theorem 3.3)

For any stopping time
\kappa\in[t,T_{f}]
, the Dynamic Programming Principle (DPP) implies

\displaystyle V(t,x)=\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda\int_{[t,\kappa]}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{[t,s)}^{t,x,U},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s-}^{t,x,U})))\mathrm{d}s+V(\kappa,\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,U}_{\kappa})\right]\;.

(88)

Proof of
Lemma

F.21
.

Refer to
Touzi (2012, Section 3.2)
.
∎

The expression of
V
given in Lemma
F.21
leads us to the following HJB equation, that in fact coincides with the one derived in
Proposition

F.16
, and is a characterization of the optimal control to the problem (
87
).

Proposition F.22
.

Assume that
V
is continuously differentiable in time. Then, the optimal control
u^{*}
to the problem (
87
) is a Markovian, i.e.,
u_{t}^{*}(\omega_{[0,t)},x)=u_{t}^{*}(\omega_{t-},x)
for
t\in(0,T_{f}]
, and admits the following formula

\displaystyle u^{*}_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))=\mathrm{e}^{V(t,x)-V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}\quad\text{ for }\ell=1,2,\ldots,d\;,

with
V
satisfies the following HJB equation

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}V(t,x)-\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\mathrm{e}^{V(t,x)-V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}=-\lambda d\;,\\
V(T_{f},x)=g(x)=-\log\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}}{\mathrm{d}\gamma^{d}}(x)\;,\end{cases}\quad\text{for }(t,x)\in[0,T_{f})\times\mathsf{X}\;.

(89)

Proof of
Proposition

F.22
.

The proof is an adaptation of Proposition 3.5 in
Touzi (2012)
. First, the DPP formula (
88
) for
t\in[0,T_{f})
and
\kappa=t+\alpha
with
\alpha>0
leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda\int_{[t,t+\alpha]}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{[t,s)}^{t,x,U},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s-}^{t,x,U})))\mathrm{d}s+V(t+\alpha,\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,U}_{t+\alpha})-V(t,x)\right]\geqslant 0\;,

for any admissible control
U\in\mathcal{D}
. Using Itô’s formula on the process
\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,U}
with the law
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}\in\mathrm{MP}(U\overrightarrow{q})
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[

\displaystyle\int_{[t,t+\alpha]}\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{[t,s)}^{t,x,U},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s-}^{t,x,U})))\mathrm{d}s+\int_{[t,t+\alpha]}(\partial_{t}V(s,\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,U}_{s})+(U_{s}\overrightarrow{q})V_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,U}_{s}))\mathrm{d}s\Bigg]\geqslant 0\;.

Using the formula of
\overrightarrow{q}
and multiplying the both hand sides by
\frac{1}{\alpha}
and pushing
\alpha\to 0
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x)))+\partial_{t}V(t,x)+\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-V(t,x)\right]U_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\geqslant 0\;,

for any
U\in\mathcal{D}
. Taking the infimum w.r.t.
U
, we get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}V(t,x)+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x)))+[V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-V(t,x)]U_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right]\geqslant 0\;,\quad\text{for }(t,x)\in[0,T_{f})\times\mathsf{X}\;.

We prove next the equality by contradiction. Assume that there exists
(t_{0},x_{0})\in\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}V(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0})))+[V(t_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))-V(t_{0},x_{0})]U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))\right]>0\;.

Denote
\Delta V(t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0})):=V(t_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))-V(t_{0},x_{0})
. The previous inequality implies that there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}V(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U)+U\Delta V\right](t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))\geqslant\varepsilon>0\;.

(90)

Take
\xi>0
small enough such that

\displaystyle\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V+\xi}-\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V})(t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\;,

(91)

and define the function
f\leqslant V
as

\displaystyle f(t,x):=V(t,x)-\xi\left[|t-t_{0}|^{2}+\updelta_{\left\{x_{0}\right\}}(x)\right]\;,\quad\text{for }(t,x)\in\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}\;.

It is clear that

\displaystyle f(t_{0},x_{0})=V(t_{0},x_{0})\;,\quad\partial_{t}f(t_{0},x_{0})=\partial_{t}V(t_{0},x_{0})\;,\quad\text{and}\quad f(t_{0},x)-V(t_{0},x)=-\xi\text{ for }x\neq x_{0}\;.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0})))+[f(t_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))-f(t_{0},x_{0})]U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}V(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0})))+\left[V(t_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))-V(t_{0},x_{0})-\xi\right]U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}V(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U)+(\Delta V-\xi)U\right](t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))\;.

The minimum above is attained at
U
such that
U(t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))=\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V+\xi}(t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))
, thus

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\partial_{t}f(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0})))+[f(t_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))-f(t_{0},x_{0})]U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))\right]

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}V(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V+\xi})+(\Delta V-\xi)\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V+\xi}\right](t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}V(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V+\xi})(t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}V(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V})(t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))+\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V}-\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V+\xi})(t_{0},x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))

\displaystyle>\varepsilon-\dfrac{\varepsilon}{2}=\dfrac{\varepsilon}{2}>0\;,

where the last inequality relies on (
91
) and (
90
) with
U=\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta V}
. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f(t_{0},x_{0})+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0})))+[f(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))-f(t_{0},x_{0})]U_{t_{0}}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))\right]>0\;.

From the continuity in time of the Hamiltonian, the previous inequality yields that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f(t,x)+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x)))+[f(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f(t,x)]U_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right]\geqslant 0\;,

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt\text{for }(t,x)\in(t_{0}-r,t_{0}+r)\times\left\{x_{0}\right\}\;,

(92)

for some
0<r<1
. Defining the stopping time
\kappa^{U}
as

\displaystyle\kappa^{U}:=\inf\left\{t\in(t_{0},T_{f}]:\overleftarrow{X}_{t-}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}\neq x_{0}\right\}\wedge(t_{0}+r)\;,

for an arbitrary control
U
, we have

\displaystyle f(\kappa^{U},\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},u}_{\kappa^{U}})=\begin{cases}f(t_{0}+r,\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}_{t_{0}+r})\;,\quad&\text{if }\quad\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}_{\kappa^{U}-}=x_{0}\;,\\
f(\kappa^{U},\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}_{\kappa^{U}})\;,\quad&\text{if }\quad\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}_{\kappa^{U}-}\neq x_{0}\;.\end{cases}

This implies that

\displaystyle f(\kappa^{U},\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},u}_{\kappa^{U}})-V(\kappa^{U},\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},u}_{\kappa^{U}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\hskip 28.45274pt-\xi r^{2}\;,\quad&\text{if }\quad\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},u}_{\kappa^{U}-}=x_{0}\;,\\
-\xi(|\kappa^{U}-t_{0}|^{2}+1)\;,\quad&\text{if }\quad\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},u}_{\kappa^{U}-}\neq x_{0}\;,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leqslant-\xi r^{2}\;.

Note that for any
s\in[t_{0},\kappa^{U}]
, not only
\overleftarrow{X}_{s-}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}=x_{0}
, thus
\overleftarrow{X}_{[t_{0},s)}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}=x_{0}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{[t_{0},\kappa^{U}]}\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{[t_{0},s)}^{t_{0},x_{0},U},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s-}^{t_{0},x_{0},U})))\mathrm{d}s+V(\kappa^{U},\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}_{\kappa^{U}})\right]

\displaystyle\geqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{[t_{0},\kappa^{U}]}\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{s}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0})))\mathrm{d}s+f(\kappa^{U},\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}_{\kappa^{U}})+\xi r^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{[t_{0},\kappa^{U}]}\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{s}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0})))\mathrm{d}s+f(\kappa^{U},\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}_{\kappa^{U}})-f(t_{0},x_{0})\right]+f(t_{0},x_{0})+\xi r^{2}\;.

Using Itô’s formula to compute the difference
f(\kappa^{U},\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}_{\kappa^{U}})-f(t_{0},x_{0})
, and relies on the fact that
V(t_{0},x_{0})=f(t_{0},x_{0})
, the calculation follows

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{[t_{0},\kappa^{U}]}\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{s}(\overleftarrow{X}_{[t_{0},s)}^{t_{0},x_{0},U},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{s-}^{t_{0},x_{0},U})))\mathrm{d}s+V(\kappa^{U},\overleftarrow{X}^{t_{0},x_{0},U}_{\kappa^{U}})\right]

\displaystyle\geqslant\mathbb{E}\int_{[t_{0},\kappa^{U}]}\Bigg[\partial_{t}f(s,x_{0})+\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(U_{s}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0})))

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+(f(s,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))-f(s,x_{0}))U_{s}(x_{0},\varphi^{(\ell)}(x_{0}))\Bigg]\mathrm{d}s+V(t_{0},x_{0})+\xi r^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{v(t,x_0)}}{\geqslant}V(t_{0},x_{0})+\xi r^{2}\;.

Since the above control
U
is arbitrary, the previous inequality is indeed a contradiction to DPP formula (
88
).

Consequently, we can deduce the following HJB equation satisfied by the value function for
(t,x)\in[0,T_{f})\times\mathsf{X}
,

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}V(t,x)+\lambda\inf_{U\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[h(U_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x)))+[V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-V(t,x)]U_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right]=0\;,\\
V(T_{f},x)=g(x)\;.\end{cases}

(93)

Proceeding as before, we minimize (
93
) and obtain the optimal control

\displaystyle u^{*}_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,u^{*}}_{[0,t)},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,u^{*}}_{t-}))=u^{*}_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,u^{*}}_{t-},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}^{t,x,u^{*}}_{t-}))=u^{*}_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))=\mathrm{e}^{V(t,x)-V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}\;,\quad

\displaystyle\text{for }\ell=1,\ldots,d\;,

that is Markovian.
Replacing the formulation of
u^{*}
into (
93
) boils down to

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}V(t,x)-\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\mathrm{e}^{V(t,x)-V(t,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}=-\lambda d\;,\\
V(T_{f},x)=g(x)=-\log\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{\star}}{\mathrm{d}\gamma^{d}}(x)\;,\end{cases}\quad\text{for }(t,x)\in[0,T_{f})\times\mathsf{X}\;,

which in fact coincides with the equation in
Proposition

F.16
and concludes the proof of Theorem
F.22
.
∎

F.5
Convergence of DMPMs

Based on the perspective of the canonical process, the backward evolution can be described as a control-driven dynamic. These tools enable us to establish the error bounds presented in
Theorem

2.3
and
Theorem

2.4
.

F.5.1
Proof of
Theorem

2.3

We first prove the curvature–dimension inequality satisfied by our forward dynamics, which is associated with the stationary distribution
\gamma^{d}=\mathrm{Uniform}(\mathsf{X})
. This serves as the key estimate for deriving the entropy decay results later.

Lemma F.23

(Curvature–dimension inequality)
.

The forward dynamic described above satisfies
curvature–dimension inequality

\mathrm{CD}(2\lambda,\infty)
, i.e.,

\Gamma_{2}(f)\geqslant 2\lambda\Gamma(f)\quad\text{ for any function }f\;,

where
\Gamma
is the carré du champ operator and
\Gamma_{2}
is the iterated carré du champ operator.

Proof of
Lemma

F.23
.

Recall the formulations of
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{2}
for functions
f
and
g
, which are typically defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Gamma(f,g)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[\overrightarrow{q}(fg)-f(\overrightarrow{q}g)-(\overrightarrow{q}f)g\right]\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma(f,f)=\Gamma(f)=\frac{1}{2}\left[\overrightarrow{q}(f^{2})-2f\overrightarrow{q}f\right]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2}(f)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[\overrightarrow{q}\Gamma(f)-2\Gamma(\overrightarrow{q}f,f)\right]\;,

where
\overrightarrow{q}
is the forward generator defined in (
14
).
These quantities capture the interaction between the functions
f
and
g
under the generator
\overrightarrow{q}
and play a crucial role in establishing results related to curvature–dimension inequalities and entropy decay.
We now compute explicitly
\Gamma(f)(x)
for
x\in\mathsf{X}
as

\displaystyle\Gamma(f)(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[\overrightarrow{q}(f^{2})-2f\overrightarrow{q}f\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{2}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(f^{2}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f^{2}(x)\right)-2f(x)\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f(x)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f(x)\right]^{2}\;.

Regarding the iterated carré du champ
\Gamma_{2}(f)
, the first term can be calculated as

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{q}\Gamma(f)(x)

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left[\Gamma(f)(\varphi^{(i)}(x))-\Gamma(f)(x)\right]

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left[\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(x)))-f(\varphi^{(i)}(x))\right)^{2}-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f(x)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[\left(f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(x)))-f(\varphi^{(i)}(x))\right)^{2}-\left(f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f(x)\right)^{2}\right]\;.

(94)

To simplify the second term of
\Gamma_{2}
, we note that

\displaystyle 2\Gamma(f,g)(x)

\displaystyle=\overrightarrow{q}(fg)-f(\overrightarrow{q}g)-g(\overrightarrow{q}f)

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))g(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f(x)g(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))+f(x)g(x)-g(x)f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right]

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[f(x)-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right]\left[g(x)-g(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right]\;.

Applying this to
g=\overrightarrow{q}f
yields

\displaystyle 2\Gamma(f,\overrightarrow{q}f)(x)

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[f(x)-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right]\left[\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(f(\varphi^{(i)}(x))-f(x)\right)-\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(x)))-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left[f(x)-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right]\left[f(\varphi^{(i)}(x))-f(x)-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(x)))+f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right]\;.

(95)

Plugging (
94
) and (
95
) into
\Gamma_{2}
yields

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2}(f)(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[\overrightarrow{q}\Gamma(f)-2\Gamma(\overrightarrow{q}f,f)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Bigg[\left(f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(x)))-f(\varphi^{(i)}(x))\right)^{2}-\left(f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f(x)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+2\left(f(x)-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right)^{2}-2\left(f(x)-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right)\left(f(\varphi^{(i)}(x))-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(x)))\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left[f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\varphi^{(i)}(x)))-f(\varphi^{(i)}(x))-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))+f(x)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[f(x)-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))+f(x)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left[f(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))-f(x)\right]^{2}=2\lambda\Gamma(f)(x)\;,\quad\text{for any }x\in\mathsf{X}\;.

Thus the desired inequality holds and we conclude the proof.

∎

We are now prepared to analyze the key distinguishing result of this paper.

Proof of
Theorem

2.3
.

We begin by establishing a bound on the “distance” between the backward path measure in continuous time,
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{*}}
, associated with the controlled process
(\overleftarrow{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]}
, and the path measure
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star}
corresponding to the simulated backward process
(\overleftarrow{X}^{\star}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]}
generated in
Algorithm

3
.
For brevity, we denote the backward path measure
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\mu^{*}}
by
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}

throughout the remainder of the paper.
By taking the stationary forward path measure
\overrightarrow{R}\in\mathrm{MP}(\overrightarrow{q})
as the reference in Girsanov’s
Theorem

F.12
, we derive the Radon–Nikodym density of
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}\in\mathrm{MP}(u\overrightarrow{q})
with respect to
\overrightarrow{R}
, where the control
u
is specified in (
84
), as follows

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}}((\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\left(\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)-\int_{\left[0,T_{f}\right]\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x))\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)\right)\;.

With a partition
0=t_{0}<...<t_{K}=T_{f}
for
K\geqslant 1
of
\left[0,T_{f}\right]
associated with the sequence of step-size
\left\{\tau_{k}\right\}_{k=1}^{K}:\tau_{k+1}=t_{k+1}-t_{k}
, the previous expression rewrites

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}}((\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigg(\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x))\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigg(\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\tilde{N}^{u\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{(u-1)\overrightarrow{q}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)-\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x))\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigg(\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\tilde{N}^{u\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\lambda\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(u_{t}-1)\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t-}))\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\lambda\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(u_{t}-\log u_{t}-1)(\mathbf{X}_{t-},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t-}))\mathrm{d}t\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigg(\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\tilde{N}^{u\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\lambda\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(u_{t}\log u_{t}-u_{t}+1)(\mathbf{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t}))\mathrm{d}t\Bigg)\;,

as
\mathbf{X}_{t-}=\mathbf{X}_{t}
for Lebesgue almost all
t\in[0,T_{f}]
.
Applying Girsanov’s theorem
F.12
once more to the path measure
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star}\in\mathrm{MP}(\hat{u}^{\theta^{\star}}\overrightarrow{q})
associated with the process
(\overleftarrow{X}^{\star}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]}
generated by
Algorithm

3
, we obtain the following expression:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}}((\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\star}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigg(\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\tilde{N}^{\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x))\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\star}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigg(\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\tilde{N}^{u\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle+\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{(u-1)\overrightarrow{q}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)-\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\varrho(\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x))\bar{\mathrm{n}}^{\overrightarrow{q}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\star}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigg(\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\tilde{N}^{u\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\lambda\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(u_{t}\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}-\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}+1)(\mathbf{X}_{t-},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t-}))\mathrm{d}t\Bigg)\;.

Since
\mathbf{X}_{t}=\mathbf{X}_{t-}
for Lebesgue almost all
t\in[0,T_{f}]
and using the fact that
\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t}))=u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}}))
for any
t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1})
,
\ell=1,\ldots,d
and
(\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]}
, the calculation follows

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}}((\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\star}}}{\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}_{0}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigg(\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)\tilde{N}^{u\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt+\lambda\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t}))\log u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}}))-u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}}))+1)\mathrm{d}t\Bigg)\;.

Combining two Radon-Nikodym densities
\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}/\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}
and
\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star}/\mathrm{d}\overrightarrow{R}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star}}((\mathbf{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})=\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}_{0}}}{\mathrm{d}\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{0}^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{0})\exp\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}(\log u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x)-\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t-},x))\tilde{N}^{u\overrightarrow{q}}_{\mathbf{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle+\lambda

\displaystyle\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\Bigg(u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t}))\log\frac{u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t}))}{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}}))}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-u_{t}(\mathbf{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t}))+u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star}}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\mathbf{X}_{t_{k}}))\Bigg)\mathrm{d}t\Bigg)\;.

This leads to the following expression of the
\mathrm{KL}
divergence

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}|\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star})=\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{T_{f}}|\gamma^{d})+\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigg[

\displaystyle\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})\times\mathsf{X}}(\log u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t-},x)-\log\hat{u}_{t}^{\theta^{\star}}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t-},x))\tilde{N}^{u\overrightarrow{q}}_{\overleftarrow{X}}(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle+\lambda

\displaystyle\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\Bigg(u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))\log\frac{u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))}{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star}}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t_{k}},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t_{k}}))}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))+u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star}}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t_{k}},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t_{k}}))\Bigg)\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]\;.

Note that
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}\in\mathrm{MP}(u\overrightarrow{q})
, thus
\tilde{N}_{\mathbf{X}}^{u\overrightarrow{q}}
is a
\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}
-martingale, which in turn allow us to reduce the first integral above. Hence we can simplify the expression for the
\mathrm{KL}
divergence as follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}|\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star})=\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{T_{f}}|\gamma^{d})+\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\Bigg[\lambda\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\Bigg(

\displaystyle u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))\log\frac{u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))}{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star}}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t_{k}},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t_{k}}))}

\displaystyle-u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))+u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star}}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t_{k}},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t_{k}}))\Bigg)\mathrm{d}t\Bigg]\;.

For brevity, we denote
u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))
as
u_{t}^{\ell}
throughout the remainder of the proof. We rewrite
\mathrm{KL}(\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}|\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star})
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}|\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{T_{f}}|\gamma^{d})+\lambda\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left({u_{t}^{\ell}}\log{u_{t}^{\ell}}-u_{t}^{\ell}\log{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}-{u_{t}^{\ell}}+{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right]\;.

We separate this expression into three terms to control:

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}|\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star})=\underbrace{\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{T_{f}}|\gamma^{d})}_{E_{1}}

\displaystyle+\lambda\underbrace{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}\log u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}-u_{t}^{\ell}\log{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}-{u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}}+{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right]}_{E_{2}}

\displaystyle+\lambda\underbrace{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left({u_{t}^{\ell}}\log{u_{t}^{\ell}}-u_{t}^{\ell}-u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}\log u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}+u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right]}_{E_{3}}\;.

(96)

We begin by observing that the uniform distribution
\gamma^{d}
is the invariant measure of the forward process, which satisfies the curvature–dimension condition
\mathrm{CD}(2\lambda,\infty)
(see
Lemma

F.23
). As a consequence, it satisfies a logarithmic Sobolev inequality by
Bakry et al. (2014, Theorem 5.10)
. This, in turn, implies exponential decay of entropy over time by
Bakry et al. (2014, Theorem 5.12)
, and thus we obtain:

E_{1}=\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{T_{f}}|\gamma^{d})\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-4\lambda T_{f}}\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\gamma^{d})\;.

(97)

Next, we bound
E_{2}
using the tower property, the martingale property established in
Proposition

F.17
, and the approximation error assumption in
Assumption

2.1
,

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}\log u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}-\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[u_{t}^{\ell}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t_{k}}\right]\log{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}-{u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}}+{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\hskip-25.6073pt\overset{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{prop:3}}{=}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}\log u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}-u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}\log{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}-{u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}}+{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}\log\frac{u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}}{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}-{u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}}+{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}h\left(\frac{u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}}{u_{t_{k}}^{\theta^{\star},\ell}}\right)\right]\;,

where
h(a)=a\log a-a+1
for
a>0
and
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]}
denotes the right-continuous and complete natural filtration generated by the process
(\overleftarrow{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]}
. Using
Assumption

2.1
, we can now bound the term
E_{2}
as

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\epsilon\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})=\epsilon(t_{K}-t_{0})=\epsilon T_{f}\;,

(98)

since
\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})
is a telescoping sum. It remains to control
E_{3}
. To do so, we leverage the monotonicity of the function
h
established in
Proposition

F.17
,

\displaystyle E_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[h({u_{t}^{\ell}})\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t_{k}}\right]-h(u_{t_{k}}^{\ell})\right)\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\int_{[t_{k},t_{k+1})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[h({u_{t_{k+1}}^{\ell}})\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t_{k}}\right]-h(u_{t_{k}}^{\ell})\right)\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})\left(\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h({u_{t_{k+1}}^{\ell}})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h({u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}})\right]\right)

Define
\tau=\max\{t_{k+1}-t_{k}:\;k=0,\ldots K-1\}
, the calculation follows

\displaystyle E_{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tau\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h({u_{t_{k+1}}^{\ell}})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h({u_{t_{k}}^{\ell}})\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\tau\left(\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h({u_{T_{f}}^{\ell}})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h({u_{0}^{\ell}})\right]\right)\leqslant\tau\beta_{\gamma^{d}}(\mu^{\star})\;,

(99)

since
h
is non-negative. Here, the Fisher-like information functional
\beta_{\gamma^{d}}
of the data distribution
\mu^{\star}
is defined as
\beta_{\gamma^{d}}(\mu^{\star}):=\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h({u_{T_{f}}^{\ell}})\right]
. Combining (
97
), (
98
) and (
99
), we arrive at

\mathrm{KL}(\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}|\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star})\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-4\lambda T_{f}}\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\gamma^{d})+\lambda\tau\beta_{\gamma^{d}}(\mu^{\star})+\lambda\epsilon T_{f}\;.

(100)

Finally, noting that
\mu^{*}=\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}^{u^{*}}_{T_{f}})
, we obtain the relation

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}^{\star}_{T_{f}}))=\mathrm{KL}(\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}})|\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}^{\star}_{T_{f}}))\leqslant\mathrm{KL}(\mathrm{Law}((\overleftarrow{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]})|\mathrm{Law}((\overleftarrow{X}_{t}^{\star})_{t\in[0,T_{f}]}))=\mathrm{KL}(\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}|\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{\star})\;,

where the inequality is known as
Data processing
inequality for
\mathrm{KL}
divergence
(
Nutz, 2021
, Lemma 1.6)
. Combining this with (
100
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}^{\star}_{T_{f}}))\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-4\lambda T_{f}}\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\gamma^{d})+\lambda\tau\beta_{\gamma^{d}}(\mu^{\star})+\lambda\epsilon T_{f}\;,

and successfully provide a theoretical guarantee for our generative models.
∎

F.5.2
Proof of
Theorem

2.4

We demonstrate
Theorem

2.4
through the following result:

Lemma F.24
.

Denoting
y_{t}=\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(u_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t},\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))\right]
then it holds for
t\in[0,T_{f})
,

y_{t}\lesssim\dfrac{d}{T_{f}-t}\;.

(101)

Proof of
Lemma

F.24
.

Recall the definition of
h(a)=a\log a-a+1
for
a>0
, and the connection between the optimal control
u_{t}
and the score function
s_{t}
, as provided by
Proposition

F.18
, is given by

u_{t}(x,\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))=1-s^{\ell}_{t}(x)\quad\text{for }\ell=1,\ldots,d\text{ and }(t,x)\in[0,T_{f})\times\mathsf{X}\;,

with the score function admitting a conditional expectation expression as given in (
18
),

s_{t}^{\ell}(x)=\mathbb{E}\left[\dfrac{2\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}{1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}-\dfrac{4\alpha_{T_{f}-t}(\overrightarrow{X}_{T_{f}-t}^{\ell}-\overrightarrow{X}_{0}^{\ell})^{2}}{1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}}\middle|\overrightarrow{X}_{T_{f}-t}=x\right]\;,\quad\text{with }\alpha_{t}=\mathrm{e}^{-2t}\;.

Using the above formulation of the score function, we estimate
y_{t}
as follows

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(1-s^{\ell}_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))\log(1-s^{\ell}_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))+s^{\ell}_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(1-s^{\ell}_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))(1-s^{\ell}_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t})-1)+s^{\ell}_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t})\right]\quad\text{(since $\log a\leqslant a-1$)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}(s^{\ell}_{t}(\overleftarrow{X}_{t}))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\dfrac{2\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}{1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}-\dfrac{4\alpha_{T_{f}-t}(\overrightarrow{X}_{T_{f}-t}^{\ell}-\overrightarrow{X}_{0}^{\ell})^{2}}{1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}}\middle|\overrightarrow{X}_{T_{f}-t}=x\right]\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\dfrac{2\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}{1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}-\dfrac{4\alpha_{T_{f}-t}(\overrightarrow{X}_{T_{f}-t}^{\ell}-\overrightarrow{X}_{0}^{\ell})^{2}}{1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}}\right)^{2}\middle|\overrightarrow{X}_{T_{f}-t}=x\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\dfrac{2\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}{1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}-\dfrac{4\alpha_{T_{f}-t}(\overrightarrow{X}_{T_{f}-t}^{\ell}-\overrightarrow{X}_{0}^{\ell})^{2}}{1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right]\;.

Expanding the last quantity and noting that
(\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{T_{f}-t}-\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{0})^{2}=(\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{T_{f}-t}-\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{0})^{4}
since
(\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{T_{f}-t}-\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{0})\in\left\{0,\pm 1\right\}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\dfrac{4\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}}{(1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t})^{2}}+\dfrac{16\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}(\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{T_{f}-t}-\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{0})^{2}}{(1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t})(1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2})}\left(-1+\dfrac{1}{1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\dfrac{4\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}}{(1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t})^{2}}+\dfrac{16\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{3}(\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{T_{f}-t}-\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{0})^{2}}{(1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\dfrac{4\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}}{(1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t})^{2}}+\dfrac{16\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[(\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{T_{f}-t}-\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{0})^{2}\right]}{(1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2})^{2}}\right)\;.

Note that

\mathbb{E}\left[(\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{T_{f}-t}-\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{0})^{2}\right]=\mathbb{P}\left(\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{T_{f}-t}\neq\overrightarrow{X}^{\ell}_{0}\right)=\dfrac{1}{2}(1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t})\;.

Thus the upper bound of
y_{t}
is

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\dfrac{4\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}}{(1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t})^{2}}+\dfrac{8\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{3}(1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t})}{(1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{4d\alpha^{2}_{T_{f}-t}}{(1+\alpha_{T_{f}-t})^{2}}\left[1+\dfrac{2\alpha_{T_{f}-t}}{(1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t})}\right]

\displaystyle=\dfrac{4d\alpha^{2}_{T_{f}-t}}{1-\alpha_{T_{f}-t}^{2}}\lesssim\dfrac{d}{\mathrm{e}^{4(T_{f}-t)}-1}\lesssim\dfrac{d}{T_{f}-t}\;,\quad\text{for }t\in[0,T_{f})\;,

where the last estimate follows from the elementary inequality
\mathrm{e}^{a}\geqslant a+1
for all
a\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore, the bound in (
101
) holds for all
t\in[0,T_{f})
.

∎

Proof of
Theorem

2.4
.

We follow the same strategy as in the proof of
Theorem

2.3
, the only difference being the way we handle the following term in (
99
)

E_{3}:=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})\left(\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(u_{t_{k+1}})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\overleftarrow{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h(u_{t_{k}})\right]\right)=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\tau_{k+1}\left(y_{t_{k+1}}-y_{t_{k}}\right)\;.

Following precisely the argument structure in the proof of Theorem 3 from
Conforti et al. (2025)
, and fixing
T_{f}
,
a
, and
c
, we choose the sequence of step-size as

\tau_{k+1}=\begin{cases}T_{f}-t_{N-1}\;,\quad&k=N-1\;,\\
ca\;,\quad&k_{0}+k_{1}\leqslant k\leqslant k_{0}+k_{1}+k_{2}-1\;,\\
c(T_{f}-t_{k})\;,\quad&k_{0}\leqslant k\leqslant k_{0}+k_{1}-1\;,\\
c\;,\quad&0\leqslant k\leqslant k_{0}-1\;,\end{cases}

(102)

and set the number of iterations
K=k_{0}+k_{1}+k_{2}+1
, with

k_{0}=\max\left\{k\geqslant 0:T_{f}-t_{k}\geqslant 1\right\},k_{1}=\max\left\{k\geqslant 0:T_{f}-t_{k_{0}+k}\geqslant a\right\}\text{ and }k_{2}=\max\left\{k\geqslant 0:T_{f}-t_{k_{0}+k_{1}+k}\geqslant 0\right\}\;.

(103)

It is shown in
Conforti et al. (2025)
that

\begin{split}k_{0}&=\lfloor c^{-1}(T_{f}-1)\rfloor,\quad k_{1}=\lfloor\log(a/(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}}))/\log(1-c)\rfloor\lesssim\log(1/a)/c\;,\\
K-k_{0}-k_{1}&=k_{2}+1\lesssim 1/c\quad\text{and}\quad\tau_{k+1}=c(1-c)^{k-k_{0}}(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}})\quad\text{for }k\in\left\{k_{0},\ldots,k_{0}+k_{1}-1\right\}\;.\end{split}

(104)

Using (
102
) and the monotonicity of
y_{t}
established in
Proposition

F.17
, we can bound
E_{3}
as

\displaystyle E_{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\tau_{k+1}\left(y_{t_{k+1}}-y_{t_{k}}\right)

\displaystyle=\tau_{K}y_{t_{K}}+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}y_{t_{k}}(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k+1})

\displaystyle=\tau_{K}y_{t_{K}}+\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}y_{t_{k}}(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k+1})+\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{k_{0}+k_{1}}y_{t_{k}}(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k+1})

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\sum_{k=k_{0}+k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}+k_{1}+k_{2}-1}y_{t_{k}}(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k+1})+y_{t_{k_{0}+k_{1}+k_{2}}}(\tau_{K-1}-\tau_{K})

\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{y_{t_{k_{0}}}[c-c(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}})]}_{(1)}+\underbrace{c\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}y_{t_{k}}\tau_{k}}_{(2)}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\underbrace{cy_{t_{k_{0}+k_{1}}}(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}-a)}_{(3)}+\underbrace{y_{t_{K}}\tau_{K-1}}_{(4)}\;.

We now bound
(1)-(2)-(3)-(4)
one-by-one. We start with

\displaystyle(1):y_{t_{k_{0}}}[c-c(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}})]\leqslant cy_{t_{k_{0}}}\overset{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{lem:2}}{\lesssim}\dfrac{cd}{T_{f}-t_{k_{0}}}\overset{\eqref{eq:def_k0k1k2}}{\leqslant}cd\;.

Next, we bound the second term

\displaystyle(2):c\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}y_{t_{k}}\tau_{k}

\displaystyle\overset{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{lem:2}}{\lesssim}c\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}\dfrac{d\tau_{k}}{T_{f}-t_{k}}=c^{2}d\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}\dfrac{\tau_{k}}{\tau_{k+1}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_k0k1k2}}{=}c^{2}d\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}\dfrac{c(1-c)^{k-k_{0}-1}(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}})}{c(1-c)^{k-k_{0}}(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}})}

\displaystyle\leqslant c^{2}d\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}\dfrac{1}{1-c}\overset{c\leqslant 1/2}{\lesssim}c^{2}dk_{1}\lesssim cd\log(1/a)\;.

Recall that
T_{f}-t_{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}\leqslant 1
. As a result, we have

\displaystyle(3):cy_{t_{k_{0}+k_{1}}}(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}-a)\overset{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{lem:2}}{\lesssim}\dfrac{cd}{T_{f}-t_{k_{0}+k_{1}}}(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}+k_{1}-1})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{cd}{T_{f}-t_{k_{0}+k_{1}-1}-\tau_{k_{0}+k_{1}}}(T_{f}-t_{k_{0}+k_{1}-1})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:step-size}}{\leqslant}\dfrac{cd}{1-c}\overset{c\leqslant 1/2}{\lesssim}cd\;.

Finally, for the last term, we have by definition of
L=y_{T_{f}}/d
,

(4):y_{t_{K}}h_{K-1}=y_{T_{f}}ca=cadL\;.

Plugging all the bounds of
(1)-(2)-(3)-(4)
into
E_{3}
gives

E_{3}\lesssim cd[1+\log(1/a)+aL]\;.

Moreover, choosing
a=1/L
yields the following bound on the sampling error

\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}^{\star}_{T_{f}}))\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-4\lambda T_{f}}\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\gamma^{d})+\lambda cd[1+\log(L)]+\lambda\epsilon T_{f}\;,

and this concludes the proof of
Theorem

2.4
.

∎

F.5.3
Proof of
Corollary

2.5

Proof of
Corollary

2.5
.

Applying
Theorem

2.4
with
c,T_{f}
chosen in (
32
) implies

\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}^{\star}_{T_{f}}))\lesssim\epsilon+\epsilon\log\dfrac{\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\gamma^{d})}{\epsilon}\;.

Therefore we obtain the approximation error
\tilde{O}(\epsilon\log(\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\gamma^{d}))
. In addition, the number of iterations is given by

\begin{split}K=k_{0}+k_{1}+K-k_{0}-k_{1}&\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_k0k1k2}}{\lesssim}\dfrac{T_{f}-1}{c}+\dfrac{\log(1/a)}{c}+\dfrac{1}{c}=\dfrac{T_{f}+\log(L)}{c}\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq:def_c_Tf}}{=}\dfrac{\lambda d[1+\log(L)][\log(\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\gamma^{d})/\epsilon)/\lambda+\log(L)]}{\epsilon}\\
&=\frac{d[1+\log(L)][\log(\mathrm{KL}(\mu^{\star}|\gamma^{d})/\epsilon)+\lambda\log(L)]}{\epsilon}\;,\end{split}

which grows logarithmically rather than linearly with respect to the discrete Fisher information
\beta_{\gamma^{d}}(\mu^{\star})
. We thus conclude that this step-size sequence offers improved performance compared to the constant step-size.

∎

F.6
Convergence of DMPMs with early stopping strategy

F.6.1
Proof of
Theorem

2.6

Proof of
Theorem

2.6
.

Applying the proof of
Theorem

2.4
with the target distribution
\mu_{\eta}
in place of
\mu^{*}
leads to the following analogous result:

\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\eta}|\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}-\eta}^{\star}))\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-4\lambda(T_{f}-\eta)}\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\eta}|\gamma^{d})+\lambda cd[1+\log(L)]+\lambda\epsilon(T_{f}-\eta)\;.

(105)

Recall that the forward dynamic satisfies the curvature–dimension condition
\mathrm{CD}(2\lambda,\infty)
(see
Lemma

F.23
) and consequently, the logarithm Sobolev inequality holds
(
Bakry et al., 2014
, Theorem 5.10)
:

\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\eta}|\gamma^{d})\leqslant\frac{1}{4\lambda}\mathcal{E}\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}},\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}}\right)\;.

(106)

As computed in
Lemma

F.23
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}},\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda\sum_{x\in\mathsf{X}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}}(x)\left(\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}}(x)-\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\right)\gamma^{d}(x)

\displaystyle=\lambda\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}\right)\right]\;.

(107)

On the other hand, the discrete Fisher information of
\mu_{\eta}
is given by

\displaystyle\beta_{\gamma^{d}}(\mu_{\eta})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h\left(\mathrm{e}^{-\log\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})\right)+\log\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))\right)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h\left(\mathrm{e}^{\log\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}\right)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}h\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}-\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}+1\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathsf{X}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}{\mu_{\eta}(x)}-\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))+\mu_{\eta}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathsf{X}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}{\mu_{\eta}(x)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathsf{X}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\mu_{\eta}(x)\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}(x)}{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(x))}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}\right)\right]\overset{\eqref{eq:dirichlet}}{=}\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathcal{E}\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}},\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}}{\gamma^{d}}\right)\;.

Plugging it into (
106
) implies that the discrete Fisher information dominates the
\mathrm{KL}
divergence as

\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\eta}|\gamma^{d})\leqslant\frac{1}{4}\beta_{\gamma^{d}}(\mu_{\eta})\;.

To complete the proof, it remains to bound the discrete Fisher information. To this end, we employ the elementary inequality
\log a\leqslant a-1
for
a>0
, and proceed using the same reasoning as in
Lemma

F.24
, as detailed below,

\displaystyle\beta_{\gamma^{d}}(\mu_{\eta})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}\log\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}-\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}+1\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}-1\right)-\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}+1\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(\frac{\mu_{\eta}(\varphi^{(\ell)}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta}))}{\mu_{\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})}-1\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\left(s^{\ell}_{T_{f}-\eta}(\overrightarrow{X}_{\eta})\right)^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{d}{T_{f}-(T_{f}-\eta)}=\dfrac{d}{\eta}\;.

It follows that

\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\eta}|\gamma^{d})\lesssim\frac{d}{\eta}\quad\text{and}\quad L=d^{-1}\beta_{\gamma^{d}}(\mu_{\eta})\lesssim\eta^{-1}\;.

Combined with (
105
), this leads to the desired conclusion:

\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\eta}|\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}-\eta}^{\star}))\lesssim d\eta^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-4\lambda(T_{f}-\eta)}+\lambda cd[1+\log(\eta^{-1})]+\lambda\epsilon(T_{f}-\eta)\;.

∎

F.6.2
Proof of
Proposition

2.7

Proof of
Proposition

2.7
.

Recall that the total variation distance between
\mu_{\eta}
and
\mu^{*}
for
\eta\in(0,\max\left\{T_{f},\frac{1}{\lambda}\right\})
is defined as

\|\mu_{\eta}-\mu^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\sum_{x\in\mathsf{X}}|\mu_{\eta}(x)-\mu^{\star}(x)|\;.

By the triangle inequality, we obtain

\|\mu_{\eta}-\mu^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leqslant\sum_{x\in\mathsf{X}}|\mu_{\eta}(x)-\mu^{\star}(x)\overrightarrow{p}_{\eta}(x,x)|+|\mu^{\star}(x)-\mu^{\star}(x)\overrightarrow{p}_{\eta}(x,x)|\;,

where the transition probability
\overrightarrow{p}_{\eta}
is defined in (
15
). The two terms above are non-negative as

\mu_{\eta}(x)=\sum_{z\in\mathsf{X}}\mu^{\star}(z)\overrightarrow{p}_{\eta}(z,x)\geqslant\mu^{\star}(x)\overrightarrow{p}_{\eta}(x,x)\;,

and the transition probability
\overrightarrow{p}_{\eta}(x,x)\leqslant 1
for any
x\in\mathsf{X}
. This together with the formula of
\overrightarrow{p}_{\eta}
in (
15
) yield

\displaystyle\|\mu_{\eta}-\mu^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{x\in\mathsf{X}}\left[\mu_{\eta}(x)+\mu^{\star}(x)-2\mu^{\star}(x)\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{e}^{-2\lambda\eta}\right)^{d}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2-2\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{e}^{-2\lambda\eta}\right)^{d}\;.

To simplify this upper bound, we apply the exponential inequality
\mathrm{e}^{a}\geqslant a+1
to
\mathrm{e}^{-2\lambda\eta}
and note that
1-\lambda\eta>0
:

\displaystyle\|\mu_{\eta}-\mu^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leqslant 2-2\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}(-2\lambda\eta+1)\right)^{d}=2-2(1-\lambda\eta)^{d}\;,

and thus the proof is complete.

∎

F.6.3
Proof of
Corollary

2.8

Proof of
Corollary

2.8
.

We observe the following by applying the triangle inequality and Pinsker’s inequality,

\displaystyle\|\mu^{\star}-\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}-\eta}^{\star})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\mu^{\star}-\mu_{\eta}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\|\mu_{\eta}-\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}-\eta}^{\star})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\mu^{\star}-\mu_{\eta}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}+\sqrt{2\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\eta}|\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}-\eta}^{\star}))}\;.

Then
Theorem

2.6
and
Proposition

2.7
together imply

\displaystyle\|\mu^{\star}-\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}-\eta}^{\star})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\lesssim 1-(1-\lambda\eta)^{d}+\sqrt{d\eta^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-4\lambda(T_{f}-\eta)}}+\sqrt{\lambda cd[1+\log(\eta^{-1})]}+\sqrt{\lambda\epsilon(T_{f}-\eta)}\;.

(108)

The choices of
\eta,c
and
T_{f}
in (
35
) lead to

1-(1-\lambda\eta)^{d}=\epsilon\quad\text{and }\quad d\eta^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-4\lambda(T_{f}-\eta)}=\epsilon^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda cd[1+\log(\eta^{-1})]=\epsilon^{2}\;.

(109)

Substituting (
109
) into (
108
) gives the desired upper bound

\displaystyle\|\mu^{\star}-\mathrm{Law}(\overleftarrow{X}_{T_{f}-\eta}^{\star})\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\lesssim\epsilon+\sqrt{\lambda\epsilon(T_{f}-\eta)}\;,

with the number of iterations is

\displaystyle K\lesssim\dfrac{T_{f}-\eta+\log(\eta^{-1})}{c}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{cond:early_stopping}}{=}\dfrac{\lambda d[1+\log(\eta^{-1})][\log(d/\eta\epsilon^{2})/\lambda+\log(\eta^{-1})]}{\epsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d[1+\log(\eta^{-1})][\log(d/\eta\epsilon^{2})+\lambda\log(\eta^{-1})]}{\epsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{d[1+\log(\eta^{-1})][\log(d/\epsilon^{2})+(\lambda+1)\log(\eta^{-1})]}{\epsilon^{2}}\;.

(110)

Finally, the term
\log\eta^{-1}
can be bounded from above by Bernoulli’s inequality
(1-\epsilon)^{1/d}\leqslant 1-\frac{\epsilon}{d}
for
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
d\geqslant 1
,

\log(\eta^{-1})=\log\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-(1-\epsilon)^{1/d}}\right)\leqslant\log\frac{\lambda d}{\epsilon}\;.

Plugging it into (
110
) concludes the proof of
Corollary

2.8
.

∎
